<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_(PP_Capuchinos)_10.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_%28PP_Capuchinos%29_10.jpg/220px-Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_%28PP_Capuchinos%29_10.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_%28PP_Capuchinos%29_10.jpg/330px-Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_%28PP_Capuchinos%29_10.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_%28PP_Capuchinos%29_10.jpg/440px-Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_%28PP_Capuchinos%29_10.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="1280" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pamplona_-_Iglesia_de_San_Antonio_(PP_Capuchinos)_10.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A sculpture of Pio of Pietrelcina in the Franciscan San Antonio church in <a href="/wiki/Pamplona" title="Pamplona">Pamplona</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a></div></div></div>
<p>Pio died in 1968 at the age of 81. His health deteriorated in the 1960s, but he continued his spiritual works. On 21 September 1968, the day after the 50th anniversary of his receiving the stigmata, Padre Pio felt great fatigue.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchug1987_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchug1987-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The next day, on 22 September 1968, he was supposed to offer a <a href="/wiki/Solemn_Mass" title="Solemn Mass">Solemn Mass</a>, but feeling weak, he asked his superior if he might say a <a href="/wiki/Low_Mass" title="Low Mass">Low Mass</a> instead, as he had done daily for years. Due to a large number of pilgrims present for the Mass, Padre Pio's superior decided the Solemn Mass must proceed. Padre Pio carried out his duties but appeared extremely weak and frail. His voice was weak, and, after the Mass had concluded, he nearly collapsed while walking down the altar steps. He needed help from his Capuchin brothers. This was his last celebration of the <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a>.
</p><p>Early in the morning of 23 September 1968, Pio made his last <a href="/wiki/Sacrament_of_Reconciliation" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacrament of Reconciliation">confession</a> and renewed his Franciscan vows.<sup id="cite_ref-ewtn2_9-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ewtn2-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> As was customary, he had his <a href="/wiki/Rosary" title="Rosary">rosary</a> in his hands, though he did not have the strength to pray the <a href="/wiki/Hail_Mary" title="Hail Mary">Hail Marys</a> aloud. Till the end, he repeated the words "Gesù, Maria" (Jesus, Mary). At around 2:30&#160;a.m., he said, "I see two mothers" (taken to mean his mother and Mary).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchug1987_99-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchug1987-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>  At 2:30&#160;a.m. he died in his cell in San Giovanni Rotondo. With his last breath he whispered, "Maria!"<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERuffin1991444_1-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERuffin1991444-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
His body was buried on 26 September in a crypt in the Church of Our Lady of Grace. His <a href="/wiki/Requiem" title="Requiem">Requiem Mass</a> was attended by over 100,000 people. He had often said, "After my death, I will do more. My real mission will begin after my death."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchug1987_99-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchug1987-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The accounts of those who stayed with Padre Pio till the end state that the stigmata had completely disappeared without a scar. Only a red mark "as if drawn by a red pencil" remained on his side, but it disappeared.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchug1987_99-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchug1987-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Posthumous_veneration">Posthumous veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Posthumous veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg/220px-Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg/330px-Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg/440px-Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2957" data-file-height="2124" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Padre_Clemente_Da_Postiglione_e_San_Pio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Padre Pio with Padre Clemente Tomay, his friend and confessor</div></div></div>
<p>In 1982, the Holy See authorized the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Manfredonia" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Manfredonia">archbishop of Manfredonia</a> to open an investigation to determine whether Pio should be canonized. The investigation continued for seven years. In 1990, Pio was declared a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>, the first step in the process of canonization. The investigation, however, did not lead to any public factual clearance by the Church on his previous 'excommunication' or on the allegations that his stigmata were not of a supernatural kind. Moreover, Pio's stigmata were remarkably left out of the obligatory investigations for the canonization process, in order to avoid obstacles prohibiting a successful closure.
</p><p>Beginning in 1990, the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> debated how Padre Pio had lived his life, and in 1997 Pope John Paul II declared him <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">venerable</a>. A discussion of the effects of his life on others followed. Cases were studied such as a reported cure of an Italian woman, Consiglia de Martino, associated with Padre Pio's <a href="/wiki/Intercession" title="Intercession">intercession</a>. In 1999, on the advice of the Congregation, John Paul II declared Padre Pio blessed. A media offensive by the Capuchins was able to realise a broad acceptation of the contested saint in society.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMargry2008_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMargry2008-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After further consideration of Padre Pio's virtues and ability to do good even after his death, including discussion of another healing attributed to his intercession, John Paul II declared Padre Pio a saint on 16 June 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEZahn2002_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZahn2002-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> An estimated 300,000 people attended the canonization ceremony in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEZahn2002_96-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZahn2002-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG/220px-Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG/330px-Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG/440px-Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary_of_Saint_Pio_of_Pietrelcina" title="Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina">Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina</a> in <a href="/wiki/San_Giovanni_Rotondo" title="San Giovanni Rotondo">San Giovanni Rotondo</a></div></div></div>
<p>On 1 July 2004, John Paul II dedicated the <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary_of_Saint_Pio_of_Pietrelcina" title="Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina">Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina</a> (sometimes referred as <i>Padre Pio Pilgrimage Church</i>), built in the village of San Giovanni Rotondo to the memory of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHooper2004_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHooper2004-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 3 March 2008, the body of Pio was exhumed from his crypt, forty years after his death, so that his remains could be prepared for display. A church statement described the body as being in "fair condition". <a href="/wiki/Archbishop" title="Archbishop">Archbishop</a> Domenico Umberto D'Ambrosio, <a href="/wiki/Papal_legate" title="Papal legate">Papal legate</a> to the shrine in San Giovanni Rotondo, stated "the top part of the skull is partly skeletal but the chin is perfect and the rest of the body is well preserved".<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Archbishop D’Ambrosio also confirmed in a communiqué that "the stigmata are not visible."<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> He said that Pio's hands "looked like they had just undergone a manicure". It was hoped that <a href="/wiki/Morticians" class="mw-redirect" title="Morticians">morticians</a> would be able to restore the face so that it would be recognizable. However, because of its deterioration, his face was covered with a lifelike <a href="/wiki/Silicone" title="Silicone">silicone</a> mask.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> This mask was made from a 1968 photograph of Padre Pio's body by the London-based Gems Studio, which usually works for wax museums and ethnological museums.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Saraiva_Martins" title="José Saraiva Martins">José Saraiva Martins</a>, prefect for the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_the_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of the Saints">Congregation for the Causes of the Saints</a>, celebrated <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a> for 15,000 devotees on April 24 at the Shrine of Holy Mary of Grace, San Giovanni Rotondo, before the body went on display in a crystal, marble, and silver <a href="/wiki/Sepulcher" class="mw-redirect" title="Sepulcher">sepulcher</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Crypt" title="Crypt">crypt</a> of the monastery.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Padre Pio is wearing his brown Capuchin habit with a white silk <a href="/wiki/Stole_(vestment)" title="Stole (vestment)">stole</a> embroidered with crystals and gold thread. His hands hold a large wooden cross. 800,000 <a href="/wiki/Pilgrims" class="mw-redirect" title="Pilgrims">pilgrims</a> worldwide, mostly from Italy, made reservations to view the body up to December 2008, but only 7,200 people a day were able to file past the crystal coffin.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Officials extended the display through September 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pio's remains were placed in the church of Saint Pio, which is beside San Giovanni Rotondo. In April 2010 they were moved to a special golden "Cripta".<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A statue of Pio in <a href="/wiki/Messina" title="Messina">Messina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sicily" title="Sicily">Sicily</a>, attracted attention in 2002 when it supposedly wept tears of blood.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Saint Pio of Pietrelcina was named the patron saint of civil defence volunteers, after a group of 160 petitioned the Italian Bishops’ conference for this designation. The bishops forwarded the request to the Vatican, which gave its approval to the designation.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> He is also "less officially" known as the patron saint of stress relief and the January blues", after the Catholic Enquiry Office in London proclaimed him as such. They designated the most depressing day of the year, identified as the Monday closest to January 22, as <i>Don't Worry Be Happy Day</i>, in honor of Padre Pio's famous advice:  "Pray, hope, and don’t worry."<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_2011_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc_2011-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Padre Pio has become one of the world's most popular saints.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> There are more than 3,000 "Padre Pio Prayer Groups" worldwide, with three million members. The first St Padre Pio parish in the world was established 16 June 2002 in Kleinburg, Ontario, Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-stpp_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stpp-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> There are <a href="/wiki/Parish" title="Parish">parishes</a> in <a href="/wiki/Vineland,_New_Jersey" title="Vineland, New Jersey">Vineland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lavallette,_New_Jersey" title="Lavallette, New Jersey">Lavallette, New Jersey</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Shrine" title="Shrine">shrines</a> in <a href="/wiki/St._Padre_Pio_Shrine" title="St. Padre Pio Shrine">Buena</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Jersey" title="New Jersey">New Jersey</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Santo_Tomas,_Batangas" title="Santo Tomas, Batangas">Santo Tomas, Batangas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>, dedicated to Padre Pio. A 2006 survey by the magazine <i><a href="/wiki/Famiglia_Cristiana" title="Famiglia Cristiana">Famiglia Cristiana</a></i> found that more Italian Catholics pray to Padre Pio for <a href="/wiki/Intercession" title="Intercession">intercession</a> than to any other figure.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The remains of Saint Pio were brought to the Vatican for veneration during the 2015–2016 <a href="/wiki/Extraordinary_Jubilee_of_Mercy" title="Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy">Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy</a>. Saint Pio and Saint <a href="/wiki/Leopold_Mandic" class="mw-redirect" title="Leopold Mandic">Leopold Mandic</a> were designated as saint-confessors to inspire people to become reconciled to the Church and to God, by the confession of their sins.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
